assistant: **Question 1: nested includes**\n\nSuppose a shared file includes another shared file:\n\n```yaml\n# .troupe/troupe.yml\ncoder: file:agents/coder.yaml\n# agents/coder.yaml\nmodel: file:models/base.yaml\n# agents/models/base.yaml\nname: gpt\n```\n\nRunning `set coder.model.temperature 0.3` adds a new key whose deepest existing ancestor (`coder.model`) comes from `base.yaml`. The contract says the override goes in "the file that contains the include reference". Read literally, that's `agents/coder.yaml`, which is itself a shared file.\n\n- **(A) Follow the contract literally (my recommendation).** The override goes in the file holding the nearest include reference (`coder.yaml`), which becomes `model: {$ref: file:models/base.yaml, temperature: 0.3}` in block form. This is predictable and matches the wording. It still modifies a shared file, but one level up.\n- **(B) Root file only.** Never touch any included file: the override goes in `troupe.yml` as `coder: {$ref: …, model: {temperature: 0.3}}`. This better matches "shared files stay shared", but it goes beyond what the contract says.\n- **(C) Something else.**\n\nWhich do you want?\n\nWAITING_FOR_DISCOVERY_INPUT

assistant: ## Intent Summary: step 5 (CR-A and CR-B)\n\n**Who and when:** a theatre owner (or a harness acting for them) runs `troupe config set PATH VALUE` on a theatre whose sections come from YAML includes that are shared with other projects.\n\n**What happens:**\n\n1. **Loading mapping-form includes.** A mapping whose `$ref` value is an include string is the included content deep-merged with its other keys, the overrides. Mappings merge recursively; scalars and lists in the override replace the included value. `get`, `show` and `show --sources` all see the merged value. Under `--sources`, overridden scalars are attributed to the including file. History and reconcile treat the merged leaves like any others.\n2. **New key under an included section (CR-A).** The key is written as an override in the including file, meaning the file holding the include reference nearest the key's deepest existing ancestor (your answer to Question 1). A scalar-form include is first converted to mapping form, keeping its end-of-line comment on the key's line. Any missing intermediate mappings are created inside the override. The shared file's bytes don't change.\n3. **`set --in-include PATH VALUE` (CR-B).** A new key goes into the included file instead (the step-1 rule, so the innermost file). When no include is involved, the result is the same as without the flag.\n4. **Existing values.** An overridden value changes in its override. A value that isn't overridden changes in the file it comes from. `--in-include` has no effect on existing values.\n5. **List items.** Appending a list item, or adding a key inside a list item, is written in the file that holds the list.\n\n**Errors:**\n- A mapping-form include of a non-YAML file that has sibling keys exits 4 (contract).\n- A `$ref` whose value isn't an include string exits 4, naming the file and path (agent choice).\n- Replacing a whole include still exits 3, as in step 1.\n\n**Rule that must always hold:** without `--in-include`, adding a new key never changes a single byte of the included file that holds the key's ancestor. Every file changes only where the write-back rules require (step-1 byte preservation, TROUPE-001).\n\n**Agent choices I'll record (contract is silent):**\n- In mapping form, `$ref` is written first and overrides follow it.\n- If the include sits under a flow-style parent, conversion is written in flow style (`{$ref: file:…, k: v}`), matching the step-1 rule for new collections under a flow parent.\n- An include that is itself a list item (`- file:a.yaml`) is converted the same way, into `- $ref: file:a.yaml` with the override key on the next line at the item's indentation.\n\nDoes this match what you want? Reply yes, or tell me what to correct. Once you confirm, I'll write and commit the spec.\n\nWAITING_FOR_DISCOVERY_INPUT

The code already has a single place that decides where a `set` writes: `writeback/placement.py`, whose docstring says the new-key rule "is expected to change at step 5". So this step fits the existing structure. Here's what I propose.\n\n## Engineering Alignment (for your approval)\n\n**1. Loading.** The loader treats a mapping with a `$ref` key as an include.\n- **No other keys:** it loads exactly like the scalar form. The include remembers it was written as a mapping, so later overrides are added to that mapping instead of converting it again.\n- **With other keys:** the include's target must be a YAML file whose content is a mapping, otherwise the load exits 4 (D-1, D-7, plus the non-YAML case from the contract).\n  - The result is a *merged mapping*: a read-only combined view that keeps both sides, the included mapping and the override mapping.\n  - Nested mappings merge the same way; anything else in the override replaces the included value.\n  - Keys appear included-first, then override-only keys.\n- **Why this shape:** the merged mapping behaves like an ordinary mapping to the rest of the code. So `get`, `show`, `show --sources` (every scalar keeps its own file), history and reconcile need almost no change. Reconcile also watches every file read on both sides.\n\n**2. Where a `set` writes.** This stays decided in `placement.py`, the one place that already decides it.\n- **Existing scalar:** written in the file that holds it. That's the override when one exists, so this needs no change.\n- **List append:** written in the list's file.\n- **New key (CR-A):** find the include reference nearest the key's deepest existing ancestor.\n  - If a list lies between that reference and the ancestor, the key is inside a list item, so it goes to the file holding the list (step-1 rule).\n  - Otherwise it's an override at that reference. If part of the path already exists as an override, the key is added there. If the include is still in scalar form, it is converted first (block, flow or list item; D-2…D-5).\n- **`--in-include` (CR-B):** uses the step-1 rule, preferring the included side of a merged mapping.\n- **Refusals:** a `$ref` segment in PATH exits 3 (D-6), and replacing a whole include of either form exits 3, as before.\n\n**3. Safety net.** The existing check that re-loads the result in memory before writing stays in place, so a write that doesn't read back exactly as intended is refused with exit 3. It gets one allowance: along the path being set, a scalar-form include may turn into mapping form, as long as it still points at the same target.\n\n**4. CLI.** `set` gains `--in-include`; other subcommands reject it with exit 2 (argparse already does this). History and lineage don't change.\n\n**5. Three behaviours the code exposed that the spec doesn't cover.** All are out of contract, so they're my choices; I'll add them to the spec with reasons:\n- **D-7 correction:** an empty included file stays an empty mapping, as in step 1. My spec wrongly said it exits 4, and that would break the existing step-1 test `test_new_key_into_empty_included_file`.\n- **D-10:** replacing a whole mapping that is partly overridden (`set coder.tools '{x: 1}'` when `tools` comes from both files) exits 3 with no file changed. There's no way to write it without either editing the shared file or the old included keys coming back through the merge.\n- **D-11:** converting an include whose `file:` string spans several lines (a block scalar or a wrapped quoted string) exits 3. It can't be moved verbatim to a `$ref:` line.\n\n**6. Tests.** All tests run against real files, the real CLI and the real event store, with every file's bytes snapshotted. Two step-1 tests change their expected output because the contract changed: `test_new_key_goes_into_included_files_mapping` and `test_new_key_into_empty_included_file`. Under the new rule those keys become overrides. No new dependencies.\n\nDo you approve this alignment, including the three spec additions?\n\nWAITING_FOR_PLANNING_INPUT

tool: Write {"content": "# Implementation Plan: troupe configuration: keep shared includes shared\n\n**Branch**: `feat/troupe-config-include-overrides` (delivered to `main` by local merge) | **Date**: 2026-10-05 | **Spec**: [spec.md](spec.md)\n**Input**: Feature specification from `spec/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4/spec.md` (step 5: CR-A Overrides, CR-B In-include)\n\n## Summary\n\n- The loader recognises **mapping-form includes** (`$ref` plus overrides) and builds a **merged mapping**: a read-only view that behaves like an ordinary mapping to every reader, so `get`, `show`, `show --sources`, history and reconcile work with almost no change. The view keeps both sides (included and override) so the writer can tell where each key lives.\n- `writeback/placement.py`, the single place that decides where a `set` writes, replaces step-1 rule 2: a new key under an included section becomes an **override** at the include reference nearest the key's deepest existing ancestor, converting a scalar-form include to mapping form first. `set --in-include` keeps the step-1 rule.\n- The existing safety net (re-load in memory, check read-back and \"nothing else changed\" before writing) stays, with one allowance: on the route to PATH a scalar-form include may become mapping form pointing at the same target.\n- No new dependencies. History and lineage are unchanged.\n\n## Engineering Alignment\n\nConfirmed by the product owner on 2026-10-05 (DM 01M46GE1PATR2GJF9SGQ9NAWQ1: \"Approved. The alignment matches the contract's Overrides and In-include sections and the answers already given in this step.\"). Implementation decisions are R-1…R-8 in [research.md](research.md); the product-level choices are spec D-1…D-11.\n\n1. **Loading** (R-1, R-2). A mapping with a `$ref` key is an include. With no other keys it loads exactly like the scalar form; the include node remembers its *holder* mapping so later overrides go there without converting again. With other keys the target must be YAML whose c...[truncated 9352 chars]
tool: Write {"content": "# Research & Decisions: troupe configuration: keep shared includes shared\n\nProduct-level out-of-contract choices are spec D-1…D-11. The decisions here are about how to implement them; the product owner confirmed them in the Engineering Alignment on 2026-10-05 (DM 01M46GE1PATR2GJF9SGQ9NAWQ1).\n\n## R-1 — How a merged mapping is represented\n\n- **Decision**: `MergedMapping` is a dataclass subclass of `MappingNode` with extra fields `base: MappingNode`, `override: MappingNode` and `ref: YamlInclude | None` (set only at the top of a mapping-form include). Its `entries` are the merged view; `origin` is the override mapping's origin; `flow` is the base's (the view's shape for verification). `YamlInclude` and `TextInclude` gain `holder: MappingNode | None`, the mapping that holds them as `$ref` (None for scalar form).\n- **Rationale**: every reader (`paths.walk`, `leaves.flatten`, `yamlout`, `values.scalar_of`, `_matches`) already handles `MappingNode`, so the merged view is correct for them without new branches; every scalar keeps its own `Origin`, so `show --sources` attributes overrides to the including file for free. Writers go through `placement.py`, which always resolves a merged mapping to one concrete side.\n- **Alternatives considered**: a separate `RefInclude` node type (rejected: a new branch in ~8 modules); merging into plain `MappingNode`s without keeping sides (rejected: placement could not tell where a key lives).\n\n## R-2 — Loader rules for `$ref` (one place: `merge.py` + `loader._mapping`)\n\n- A raw mapping with a `$ref` key: its value must be a scalar whose string starts `file:`, else `LoadError` naming file and `<path>.$ref` (spec D-1). The include resolves like a scalar-form include (same relative-path, missing-target and cycle rules).\n- No siblings → return the include node with `holder` set (YAML or text).\n- Siblings → text target: `LoadError` (contract). YAML target whose unwrapped root is not a mapping: `LoadError` (D-7; an empty...[truncated 4337 chars]
tool: Write {"content": "# Data Model: keep shared includes shared\n\n## Tree nodes (`troupe/tree.py`)\n\n| Node | New / changed fields | Meaning |\n|---|---|---|\n| `YamlInclude` | `holder: MappingNode \\| None` | The mapping that holds this include as `$ref` (mapping form without overrides); `None` for scalar form. |\n| `TextInclude` | `holder: MappingNode \\| None` | Same; a text include never has overrides. |\n| `MergedMapping(MappingNode)` | `base: MappingNode`, `override: MappingNode`, `ref: YamlInclude \\| None` | Merged view: `entries` = base keys in base order (value from override where present, nested `MergedMapping` where both are mappings) then override-only keys. `ref` set only at the top of a mapping-form include; `override` is then the holder mapping (its `$ref` entry excluded from `entries`). |\n\nInvariants:\n\n- No `$ref` key appears in any merged view (`entries`) or flattened leaf path.\n- Every `ScalarNode`/`TextInclude` in the view keeps its own `Origin` (its source file).\n- A `MergedMapping` is never written through directly: placements only carry concrete `MappingNode`/`SequenceNode`s.\n\n## Placements (`troupe/writeback/placement.py`)\n\n| Placement | When | File written |\n|---|---|---|\n| `ReplaceScalar` / `ReplaceText` / `ReplaceCollection` | existing value (one-sided) | the file holding it |\n| `AppendItem` | index = list length | the list's file |\n| `AddKeys(mapping, keys)` | new key: step-1, `--in-include`, or existing override mapping | `mapping.origin.file` |\n| `ConvertInclude(include, keys)` (NEW) | new key at a scalar-form include reference | the including file |\n| refusal (exit 3) | whole include; `MergedMapping` with or without `ref`; `$ref` segment; multi-line include string | none |\n\n## Load errors (exit 4)\n\n- `$ref` value not a `file:` string (D-1).\n- Mapping-form include with siblings and a non-YAML target (contract).\n- Mapping-form include with siblings and YAML content that is not a mapping (D-7).\n- Missing target / cycle (st...[truncated 109 chars]
tool: Write {"content": "# CLI Contract additions: step 5 (Overrides, In-include)\n\nRun as `uv run troupe config <subcommand> [--theatre DIR] [ARGS]`. All step 1–4 sections stay in force except rule 2 under `set PATH VALUE`, replaced by Overrides. Source of truth: spec.md FR-001…FR-013 and D-1…D-11.\n\n## Includes: mapping form\n\n```yaml\ncoder:\n  $ref: file:agents/coder.yaml   # the include string\n  temperature: 0.3               # overrides, deep-merged over the included content\n```\n\n- Mappings merge recursively; scalars and lists in the override replace. Keys: included first, then override-only.\n- `$ref` alone behaves exactly like the scalar form (YAML or text target).\n- `get`/`show` never show `$ref`; `show --sources` names the including file for override scalars.\n\n## `set PATH VALUE [--reason TEXT] [--in-include]`\n\n| Case | Written to |\n|---|---|\n| Existing scalar, overridden | the override (including file) |\n| Existing scalar, not overridden | the file it comes from |\n| New key, deepest existing ancestor from an included file | override in the file holding the nearest include reference (scalar form converted first) |\n| New key, same, with `--in-include` | the included file (innermost that defines the ancestor) |\n| New key inside a list item; list append | the file holding the list |\n| New key, ancestor not from an include | that file (unchanged) |\n\nConversion: `coder: file:agents/coder.yaml  # c` → `coder:  # c` / `  $ref: file:agents/coder.yaml` / `  temperature: 0.3` (file indentation; flow parent → `{$ref: …, k: v}`; list item → `- $ref: …` with overrides at the item column).\n\n## Exit codes (additions)\n\n| Code | Cases |\n|---|---|\n| 2 | `--in-include` on any subcommand other than `set` |\n| 3 | PATH with a `$ref` segment (D-6); replacing a partly overridden mapping (D-10); conversion of a multi-line include string (D-11); replacing a whole include of either form (unchanged) |\n| 4 | `$ref` value not a `file:` string (D-1); mapping-form includ...[truncated 316 chars]
tool: Write {"content": "# Quickstart: overrides and `--in-include`\n\n```bash\nT=$(mktemp -d); mkdir -p $T/.troupe/agents\nprintf 'coder: file:agents/coder.yaml  # c\\n' > $T/.troupe/troupe.yml\nprintf 'model: gpt\\ntemperature: 0.7\\n' > $T/.troupe/agents/coder.yaml\n\nuv run troupe config set coder.top_p 0.9 --theatre $T\ncat $T/.troupe/troupe.yml          # coder:  # c / $ref: file:agents/coder.yaml / top_p: 0.9\ncat $T/.troupe/agents/coder.yaml   # unchanged\n\nuv run troupe config set coder.temperature 0.5 --theatre $T   # not overridden: changes coder.yaml\nuv run troupe config set coder.top_p 0.8 --theatre $T         # overridden: changes troupe.yml\n\nuv run troupe config set --in-include coder.max_tokens 100 --theatre $T   # appended to coder.yaml\nuv run troupe config show coder --sources --theatre $T\nuv run troupe config history --theatre $T\n```\n\nExpected `show coder --sources`: `model`, `temperature`, `max_tokens` from `.troupe/agents/coder.yaml`; `top_p` from `.troupe/troupe.yml`.\n", "file_path": "/work/repo/spec/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4/quickstart.md"}

CR-A and CR-B are both complete after WP04.\n- **Profiles:** every package is assigned `python-pedro` (implementer, claude).\n\n**Design details refined while writing the packages.** These are in research and the data model (`6072099`); behaviour is unchanged:\n- The nearest include reference is found by matching the include whose target is the file holding the deepest existing ancestor.\n- A merged mapping keeps both sides as found.\n- Writes only ever go through a real parent mapping or list in the file that holds the value, never the merged view.\n\n**Sequencing caveat:** once WP03 lands, the two step-1 tests that change under the new contract would fail until WP04 lands. tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"The deep-merge rule for mapping-form includes: the one place it is decided (FR-002, R-2).\n\nA mapping-form include (``coder: {$ref: file:agents/coder.yaml, temperature: 0.3}``) reads as\nthe included content with the holder's other keys (*overrides*) deep-merged over it. This module\nis pure: it builds a ``MergedMapping`` view from two already-loaded sides and reads no files.\n``troupe.loader`` recognises ``$ref`` and calls ``deep_merge``; nothing else merges.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom typing import Final\n\nfrom troupe.paths import unwrap\nfrom troupe.tree import MappingEntry, MappingNode, MergedMapping, Node, Origin, YamlInclude\n\nREF_KEY: Final = \"$ref\"\n\n\ndef deep_merge(\n    base: Node, override: Node, *, ref: YamlInclude | None, origin: Origin, flow: bool\n) -> MergedMapping:\n    \"\"\"Merge ``override`` over ``base`` (both as found; both unwrap to mappings).\n\n    Rules (contract *Overrides*; spec FR-002, D-7; research R-2):\n\n    - A key only in the base keeps the base entry; a key only in the override keeps the override\n      entry. A ``$ref`` key is never data and never appears in the view (D-1).\n    - A key in both whose values both unwrap to mappings merges recursively into a nested\n      ``MergedMapping`` (``ref=None``), under the override entry's key text, key origin and key\n      style.\n    - Otherwise the override entry replaces the base entry: scalars and lists in the override\n      replace, and so does a mapping over a scalar or a scalar over a mapping.\n    - Key order: the base's keys in base order (each at its base position, even when\n      overridden), then override-only keys in override order (spec Assumptions).\n\n    ``MappingEntry`` objects and value nodes of both sides are reused, never copied: callers rely\n    on identity to find the concrete side a node lives in.\n\n    Args:\n        base: The included side as found (usually a ``YamlInclude``).\n        override: The override side as found (the holder mapping a...[truncated 1892 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"``merge.deep_merge`` on trees the real loader builds from real files (FR-002, research R-2).\n\nEach test writes a theatre whose ``troupe.yml`` holds a mapping-form include of\n``agents/coder.yaml`` and inspects the ``MergedMapping`` the loader returns for ``coder``.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom conftest import MakeTheatre\nfrom troupe.loader import load\nfrom troupe.merge import REF_KEY\nfrom troupe.paths import unwrap\nfrom troupe.theatre import locate\nfrom troupe.tree import MappingNode, MergedMapping, Node, ScalarNode, SequenceNode, YamlInclude\n\nCODER = \"model: gpt\\ntemperature: 0.7\\ntools:\\n  shell: true\\nstop: [a, b]\\n\"\n\n\ndef _coder(make_theatre: MakeTheatre, overrides: str, coder: str = CODER) -> MergedMapping:\n    \"\"\"Load a theatre with ``coder: {$ref: ..., <overrides>}``; return the merged ``coder``.\"\"\"\n    theatre = make_theatre(\n        {\n            \"troupe.yml\": \"coder:\\n  $ref: file:agents/coder.yaml\\n\" + overrides,\n            \"agents/coder.yaml\": coder,\n        }\n    )\n    root = load(locate(theatre).config_file)\n    assert isinstance(root, MappingNode)\n    entry = root.get(\"coder\")\n    assert entry is not None\n    assert isinstance(entry.value, MergedMapping)\n    return entry.value\n\n\ndef _value(mapping: MappingNode, key: str) -> Node:\n    entry = mapping.get(key)\n    assert entry is not None, key\n    return entry.value\n\n\ndef _scalar(mapping: MappingNode, key: str) -> ScalarNode:\n    node = _value(mapping, key)\n    assert isinstance(node, ScalarNode)\n    return node\n\n\ndef _included_root(merged: MergedMapping) -> MappingNode:\n    \"\"\"The included file's own tree (the base side, unwrapped).\"\"\"\n    assert isinstance(merged.base, YamlInclude)\n    root = unwrap(merged.base)\n    assert isinstance(root, MappingNode)\n    return root\n\n\ndef test_override_scalar_replaces_included_scalar(make_theatre: MakeTheatre) -> None:\n    merged = _coder(make_theatre, \"...[truncated 4478 chars]

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Loading mapping-form includes (``$ref``) through the real CLI: FR-001, FR-003, spec D-1/D-7.\n\nSpec US1 scenarios 5 and 6 and the Edge Cases. Every refusal is checked the same way: exit 4,\nempty stdout, one stderr line ``troupe: ...`` naming the file, and no theatre file changed.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom conftest import MakeTheatre, run_cli, snapshot\n\ntype Files = dict[str, str | bytes]\n\nCODER = \"model: gpt\\ntools:\\n  shell: true\\nstop: [a, b]\\n\"\nPROMPT = \"Be brief.\\nReally.\\n\"\n\n\ndef _cli(theatre: Path, *args: str) -> tuple[int, str, str]:\n    result = run_cli(\"config\", *args, \"--theatre\", str(theatre))\n    return result.code, result.out, result.err\n\n\ndef _reads(theatre: Path) -> list[tuple[int, str, str]]:\n    \"\"\"Every read the step offers on the include at ``coder``/``prompt``, plus ``show``.\"\"\"\n    return [\n        _cli(theatre, \"show\"),\n        _cli(theatre, \"show\", \"--sources\"),\n        _cli(theatre, \"show\", \"coder\"),\n        _cli(theatre, \"get\", \"coder.model\"),\n        _cli(theatre, \"get\", \"coder.stop.1\"),\n    ]\n\n\ndef _assert_load_refused(theatre: Path, *names: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Run a loading command; assert exit 4, no stdout, one ``troupe:`` line naming ``names``.\"\"\"\n    before = snapshot(theatre)\n    code, out, err = _cli(theatre, \"show\")\n    assert (code, out) == (4, \"\")\n    assert err.startswith(\"troupe: \") and err.endswith(\"\\n\") and err.count(\"\\n\") == 1, err\n    for name in names:\n        assert name in err, (name, err)\n    assert snapshot(theatre) == before\n    return err\n\n\n@pytest.mark.parametrize(\n    (\"scalar_form\", \"mapping_form\"),\n    [\n        (\"coder: file:agents/coder.yaml\\n\", \"coder:\\n  $ref: file:agents/coder.yaml\\n\"),\n        (\"{coder: file:agents/coder.yaml}\\n\", \"{coder: {$ref: file:agents/coder.yaml}}\\n\"),\n    ],\n    ids=[\"block\", \"flow\"],\n)\ndef test_ref_alone_read...[truncated 4698 chars]

The number in a test's comment is the US1 acceptance scenario it covers.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport time\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom conftest import (\n    MakeTheatre,\n    WatchProcess,\n    core,\n    history_events,\n    recorded_within,\n    run_cli,\n    snapshot,\n)\nfrom troupe.loader import load\nfrom troupe.reconcile import watched_files\nfrom troupe.theatre import locate\n\ntype Files = dict[str, str | bytes]\ntype Event = dict[str, object]\n\nROOT = \"coder:\\n  $ref: file:agents/coder.yaml\\n  temperature: 0.3\\n  tools:\\n    web: true\\n\"\nCODER = \"model: gpt\\ntemperature: 0.7\\ntools:\\n  shell: true\\nstop: [a, b]\\n\"\nFIXTURE: Files = {\"troupe.yml\": ROOT, \"agents/coder.yaml\": CODER}\nIMPORTED = 6  # merged leaves in FIXTURE, so the first event after the import has seq 7\n\n\ndef _cli(theatre: Path, *args: str) -> tuple[int, str, str]:\n    result = run_cli(\"config\", *args, \"--theatre\", str(theatre))\n    return result.code, result.out, result.err\n\n\ndef _write(theatre: Path, rel: str, text: str) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Hand-edit ``.troupe/<rel>`` and return ``time.monotonic()`` right after the write.\"\"\"\n    (theatre / \".troupe\" / rel).write_bytes(text.encode(\"utf-8\"))\n    return time.monotonic()\n\n\ndef _event(seq: int, op: str, path: str, old: object, new: object) -> Event:\n    return {\"seq\": seq, \"op\": op, \"path\": path, \"old\": old, \"new\": new}\n\n\ndef test_get_returns_override_where_present_else_included_value(\n    make_theatre: MakeTheatre,\n) -> None:  # 1\n    theatre = make_theatre(FIXTURE)\n\n    assert [\n        _cli(theatre, \"get\", \"coder.temperature\"),\n        _cli(theatre, \"get\", \"coder.model\"),\n        _cli(theatre, \"get\", \"coder.tools.web\"),\n        _cli(theatre, \"g...[truncated 4548 chars]

assistant: WP01 is finished and moved to `for_review`. All four gates pass. `merge.deep_merge` is called from `loader.py:188` and recursively from itself, and nowhere else.\n\n**What the code does now**\n- **Tree nodes (T001):** `YamlInclude` and `TextInclude` have a `holder` field, set on every include written as a `$ref` value, with or without sibling keys. `MergedMapping` is a new subclass of `MappingNode` with `base`, `override` and `ref`.\n- **Merge rule (T002):** it lives only in the new `src/troupe/merge.py`. Nested mappings merge recursively; scalars, lists and mismatched kinds are replaced by the override. Key order is the included file's keys first, then override-only keys. Entry and node objects are reused, never copied, and `$ref` never appears in the merged view.\n- **Loader (T003):** a mapping with a `$ref` key (any quoting) is an include. The `$ref` value is converted once and reused, so the target is never loaded twice. With no sibling keys it returns the include node exactly as the scalar form does. Three new exit-4 errors name the file and path: a `$ref` value that is not a `file:...` string, overrides on a non-YAML target, and overrides on a target whose content is a list or scalar. An empty target accepts overrides.\n- **Watched files (T004):** `watched_files` follows both sides of a merge, so `watch` sees the `$ref` target and includes hidden by an override. `paths.py` only got a docstring change.\n\n**One deviation from the prompt:** `holder` is declared as `field(default=None, compare=False, repr=False)`. The holder's `$ref` entry points back at the include, so including it in `==` would recurse forever when comparing two separately loaded but equal trees. It still defaults to `None`, so every existing constructor call works unchanged.\n\n**Tests added (34 runs from 26 functions, all real files; integration tests use the real CLI)**\n- `tests/unit/test_merge.py`: all 8 tests the prompt names.\n- `tests/integration/test_loading_ref.py`: all 9 tests the prompt names (17 runs with parametrization). Each exit-4 test checks exit 4, empty stdout, one `troupe: ` stderr line naming the file, and no theatre file changed.\n- `tests/integration/test_reads_overrides.py`: all 8 tests the prompt names, plus one extra, `test_watched_files_include_ref_target_and_includes_hidden_by_overrides`, to cover the reviewer check that a hidden include is still watched.\n\n**Gates (final run, from the worktree)**\n- `uv run pytest -q` → `1686 passed in 411.47s` (the previous 1652 plus the 34 new runs)\n- `uv run mypy --strict` → `Success: no issues found in 71 source files`\n- `uv run ruff check` → `All checks passed!`\n- `uv run ruff format --check` → `75 files already formatted`\n\n**Commit and status**\n- Commit `67ddc0c` "feat(WP01): mapping-form includes load into merged views", ending with the Co-Authored-By line; `__pycache__` was removed before staging.\n- `mark-status`: "Marked 7 subtasks as done: T001…T007".\n- `align-lane.sh` added commit `0b4fd6c` "chore: align lane spec with planning branch".\n- `move-task WP01 --to for_review` succeeded without `--force`. It noted that WP03 depends on WP01 and would need a rebase onto lane-a if review asks for changes.\n\nNothing in the prompt was impossible or contradicted the code.\n\n**Files** (under `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-a/`):\n- `src/troupe/tree.py`\n- `src/troupe/merge.py` (new)\n- `src/troupe/loader.py`\n- `src/troupe/reconcile.py`\n- `src/troupe/paths.py`\n- `tests/unit/test_merge.py` (new)\n- `tests/integration/test_loading_ref.py` (new)\n- `tests/integration/test_reads_overrides.py` (new)

assistant: WP02 is done and in `for_review`. `convert_include_edits` turns a scalar-form include into mapping form with its first override. The contract example comes out byte-exact (`coder:  # c\n  $ref: file:agents/coder.yaml\n  temperature: 0.3\n`), and all four gates pass. Nothing calls the function yet; that's expected, since WP04 wires it in from `apply.py` after WP03's placement.\n\n**Files changed** (only the two this WP owns):\n- `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-b/src/troupe/writeback/collections.py`\n  - Adds `convert_include_edits` with the signature from the prompt, plus a `REF_KEY` constant. It uses only the existing types.\n  - Small refactors so the step-1 renderers are shared rather than copied: `_add_new_keys` (taken out of `new_key_edits`), `_flow_entries` (taken out of `render_flow_value`) and `_insert_lines` (taken out of `_insert_entry`). Behaviour is unchanged and the existing tests are untouched and pass.\n- `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-b/tests/unit/test_collections.py`\n\n**Tests added:** 28 test cases under the 14 required test names. Each loads a real theatre built with `make_theatre`, applies the edits with `apply_edits`, compares the exact resulting string and checks the result still parses as YAML. They cover:\n- the contract example\n- no comment: kept trailing spaces, and a file without a final line break\n- a 4-space file indenting by 4\n- the value on the line after the key\n- intermediate keys becoming nested mappings\n- list, mapping, multi-line string, empty-list and quoted values\n- flow mapping, including keeping the comment column and a multi-line string written double-quoted\n- flow list\n- block list items: indented, flush, and with a wide gap after the dash\n- whole-document include, with and without a BOM\n- single- and double-quoted include strings kept verbatim\n- a CRLF file staying CRLF\n- refusal of a wrapped double-quoted include and a `|-` block include: exit code 3 and the exact message\n- a line-by-line check that nothing else changes\n\nI also broke the implementation on purpose once and confirmed the tests fail.\n\n**Gates** (run from the lane worktree):\n- `uv run pytest -q`: `1680 passed in 391.76s`\n- `uv run mypy --strict`: `Success: no issues found in 67 source files`\n- `uv run ruff check`: `All checks passed!` (after fixing one import-order finding)\n- `uv run ruff format --check`: `71 files already formatted`\n\nThe full pytest run happened just before that import-order fix; afterwards I re-ran `test_collections.py` (201 passed) and the other three gates.\n\n**Commit:** `7e6c7fe` "feat(WP02): convert_include_edits turns a scalar-form include into mapping form". The lane-alignment script then added `5a67d31`. The worktree is clean.\n\n**Status updates:** `mark-status` marked T008–T012 done. `move-task WP02 --to for_review` succeeded with no lane-guard refusal. It printed a reminder that WP04 depends on WP02.\n\n**Where the prompt contradicted itself:**\n1. **Comment spacing on list items (T010).** The prompt says to keep the comment's column (FR-020, via `replace_in_line`), but gives the result `- $ref: file:a.yaml  # c`, which needs the original two-space gap. The FR-020 rule would produce `- $ref: file:a.yaml # c` with one space. I followed the given result and spec D-5 (keep the bytes between the include string and the comment) by inserting `$ref: ` in front of the string. I did the same for the value-on-next-line and whole-document cases, and documented this in the docstring. Flow parents do keep the comment column as the prompt says.\n2. **Quoting style for override values.** The new override lines don't copy quoting from the `$ref` value, so a quoted include string doesn't make the override quoted. The spec doesn't say whether `$ref` counts as a sibling for FR-015; I treated it as not being a configuration value.\n3. **Checking the result by reloading.** These tests deliberately don't reload the converted text through the loader. After WP01 merges, the loader will read `$ref` mappings with merge semantics and such checks would break. They compare exact strings and confirm the result parses instead.

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-b && cat > /tmp/probe_wp02.py <<'EOF'\nimport sys\nsys.path.insert(0, \"tests\")\nfrom pathlib import Path\nimport tempfile\nfrom troupe.loader import load\nfrom troupe.tree import MappingNode, SequenceNode, YamlInclude\nfrom troupe.writeback import apply_edits\nfrom troupe.writeback.collections import convert_include_edits, detect_indent\nfrom troupe.errors import WriteRefused\nfrom troupe import yamlcore\n\ndef run(before, at, keys, value):\n    d = Path(tempfile.mkdtemp()); t = d/\".troupe\"; (t/\"agents\").mkdir(parents=True)\n    (t/\"troupe.yml\").write_bytes(before.encode()); (t/\"agents/coder.yaml\").write_text(\"model: m\\n\"); (t/\"agents/a.yaml\").write_text(\"n: a\\n\")\n    root = load(t/\"troupe.yml\")\n    node = root; parent=None; slot=None\n    for s in at:\n        parent = node; slot = s\n        node = node.get(s).value if isinstance(node, MappingNode) else node.items[s]\n    assert isinstance(node, YamlInclude), type(node)\n    try:\n        e = convert_include_edits(node.origin.file, parent, slot, node.origin, keys, value, detect_indent(before), path=\"p\")\n        a = apply_edits(before, e); print(repr(a)); yamlcore.compose(a, \"x\")\n    except WriteRefused as ex: print(\"REFUSED\", ex)\nrun(\"coder: file:agents/coder.yaml\", [\"coder\"], [\"p\"], \"a\\nb\\n\")\nrun(\"l:\\n- file:agents/a.yaml\", [\"l\", 0], [\"p\"], \"a\\nb\\n\")\nrun(\"# head\\nfile:agents/coder.yaml\\n# tail\\n\", [], [\"k\"], 1)\nrun(\"x:\\n  - coder: file:agents/coder.yaml # c\\n\", [\"x\", 0, \"coder\"], [\"t\"], 1)\nrun(\"coder: |\\n  file:agents/coder.yaml\\n\", [\"coder\"], [\"t\"], 1)\nrun(\"coder: file:agents/coder.yaml\\n# c\\nz: 1\\n\", [\"coder\"], [\"p\"], \"a\\nb\\n\")\nrun(\"coder:   'file:agents/coder.yaml'   # c\\n\", [\"coder\"], [\"t\"], 1)\nEOF\nuv run python /tmp/probe_wp02.py"}

assistant: **Verdict: approved.** WP02 has moved from in_review to approved. I found no blocking defects.\n\n**Commands run**\n- Review prompt and the full diff (`method/mission-troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4..HEAD`): I read every changed line in `collections.py` and `test_collections.py`.\n- `uv run pytest -q tests/unit/test_collections.py`: 201 passed in 2.76s.\n- `uv run mypy --strict`: Success, no issues found in 67 source files.\n- `uv run ruff check`: All checks passed.\n- `uv run ruff format --check`: 71 files already formatted.\n- `git diff --stat` excluding spec: only `src/troupe/writeback/collections.py` (+141/−10) and `tests/unit/test_collections.py` (+263) changed.\n- My own probe script, run against the real loader and then deleted (results below).\n- `align-lane.sh`: aligned, ea7f97c.\n- `move-task WP02 --to approved`: "Moved WP02 from in_review to approved".\n- I did not run the full pytest suite.\n\n**What I verified**\n- **Refactors keep behaviour the same:**\n  - `_add_new_keys` is the old loop moved as is; `indent += step` became `renderer.step`, which is the same value.\n  - `_flow_entries` is the old generator without the braces.\n  - `_insert_lines` is `_insert_entry` with the position passed in.\n  - The test diff only adds lines, so the existing collections tests are unchanged and they pass.\n- **T008–T012:** all 14 required test names exist, and each asserts what its name says.\n  - The tests load real theatre files with `troupe.loader.load`, apply the edits with `apply_edits` and compare exact strings.\n  - The contract example matches byte for byte: `coder:  # c\n  $ref: file:agents/coder.yaml\n  temperature: 0.3\n`.\n- **D-11 refusal:** it is checked before any edit is built. It covers a wrapped double-quoted string, `|-` and `>-`. The tests assert exit code 3 and the exact message, which names PATH, the file and the line.\n- **Other formatting rules:** CRLF stays CRLF, a 4-space file gets 4-space nesting, single- and double-quoted include bytes move to `$ref:` unchanged, and `test_convert_changes_no_other_line` shows only the include line changes plus the inserted lines.\n- **Probes:**\n  - A file with no final line break plus a multi-line override value gives valid output.\n  - An include as a key inside a list item (`- coder: file:… # c`) is converted at the right column.\n  - At the document root, comment lines before and after the include are kept.\n  - Comment lines after the include stay after the inserted lines.\n  - A list-valued override renders correctly.\n  - A file ending in a block scalar is not affected.\n  - All results parse as YAML.\n- **The three deviations:** I accept all of them. (1) is backed by spec D-5 and research R-7 ("replace `- <bytes>` with `- $ref: <bytes>`"). (2) is right because `$ref` is not a configuration sibling. (3) is fine because the conversion is a text-level unit.\n\n**Anti-pattern checklist**\n1. Dead code: N/A for now. `convert_include_edits` has no production caller yet; WP03 decides when to convert and WP04 wires the `set` command, so watch that WP04 actually calls it.\n2. Synthetic-fixture test: PASS. The tests call the real function on real loader output.\n3. Silent empty return: PASS. There are none.\n4. FR coverage: PASS. FR-006 and D-2, D-3, D-4, D-5 and D-11 each have assertions.\n5. Frozen surface: PASS.\n6. Locked decision: PASS.\n7. Shared-file ownership: PASS. No other WP owns `collections.py`; WP05 only reads its docstring.\n8. Production fragility: PASS. The two new `ValueError`s are for caller mistakes (no keys given, or a mapping parent without a key). `WriteRefused` is the D-11 refusal the spec asks for.\n\n**Non-blocking findings**\n- `src/troupe/writeback/collections.py`, `convert_include_edits`: it uses `_Renderer(step, step)` rather than `_sequence_offset`. That is fine, because the new mapping has no sibling list whose indentation it could copy.\n- The public constant `REF_KEY` is only used inside this function so far. WP03 or WP04 could reuse it so `$ref` is spelled in one place.\n- When the include is on the key's line, an anchor or tag in front of the include string (such as `coder: &a file:x`) would be deleted along with the value. That form is outside the spec; WP03 could refuse it if it ever matters.\n- An untracked `.method/` directory sits in the lane worktree. It was there before this review and I left it alone.\n\nFiles reviewed:\n- /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-b/src/troupe/writeback/collections.py\n- /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-b/tests/unit/test_collections.py
assistant: WP02 is approved. The review raised two things I'm carrying forward:\n- **`$ref` spelled in two places.** WP01's `merge.py` and WP02's `collections.py` each define `REF_KEY`, which goes against the one-decision-one-place rule (TROUPE-007). WP04 will make one import the other.\n- **Possible byte loss.** If someone writes an anchor or tag before an include (`coder: &a file:x`), conversion would silently delete it. That form is outside the spec, but losing bytes isn't acceptable, so WP04 or WP05 will refuse that case with exit 3. I'm tracking it as a follow-up issue so it isn't lost.

This matches the implementer's count.\n- `align-lane.sh`: **aligned: 996f697**\n- `method agent tasks move-task WP01 --to approved ...`: **Moved WP01 from in_review to approved**\n\n**What I checked**\n- **No double loading:** `_mapping` converts the `$ref` value once in `_ref_include`. It passes that same node to `_entries(..., converted={ref_index: include})`, so the target is loaded once and cycle detection is not confused.\n- **No `$ref` leaks:** `deep_merge` leaves the `$ref` entry out of the merged entries, and the unit test checks this. The test of first-run history imports (`test_first_command_imports_merged_leaves`) asserts the exact leaf paths, and none contains `$ref`.\n- **Error messages:**\n  - A bad `$ref` value names the file and `<path>.$ref`, and shows the value's source text. It is tested with all six values the WP prompt lists.\n  - Overrides on a non-YAML target name the file, the path and the target.\n  - Overrides on list or scalar content name the file and the target.\n  - Every exit-4 test checks for exit 4, empty stdout, a single `troupe: ` line, and that no file in the test directory changed.\n- **Empty target with overrides:** accepted as an empty mapping, and tested with `show --sources`.\n- **Watched files:** `_collect` handles `MergedMapping` before `MappingNode` and follows both the base and the override. An extra test, `test_watched_files_include_ref_target_and_includes_hidden_by_overrides`, shows that `agents/shared.md` (hidden by an override) is still watched.\n- **One home for the merge rule:** the rule lives only in `merge.py`, and `MappingEntry` and node objects are reused rather than copied, as the identity test confirms. The two tree nodes reach the merge only through `unwrap`.\n- **Implementer notes:** both are fine.\n  - (a) `holder` with `compare=False, repr=False` is correct. It breaks the include → holder → `$ref` entry → include loop for `==` and `repr`, and nothing compares includes by `holder`.\n  - (b) Setting `holder` whether or not there are sibling keys matches R-1 and finding I1.\n- **Test names:** all tasks T001–T007 are delivered, and each test named in the WP prompt exists and asserts what its name says.\n- **Anti-pattern checklist:** all 8 items pass.\n  - Dead code: `merge.deep_merge` and `REF_KEY` are called from `loader.py`.\n  - Synthetic fixtures: the tests use the real loader and the real CLI.\n  - Silent empty returns: none.\n  - FR coverage: FR-001 to FR-004 are each covered.\n  - Frozen files and locked decisions: no violations.\n  - Shared files: covered by a coordination note in the approval message for WP03 and WP04.\n  - Fragility: each new `raise` is a deliberate refusal that names the file, and the two `assert`s rely on the loader having checked first.\n\n**Non-blocking suggestions**\n1. `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-a/src/troupe/merge.py`: `deep_merge` drops `$ref` from the override at every nesting level, but this can only matter at the top. That is acceptable as it stands.\n\nFiles reviewed (in `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-a/`):\n- `src/troupe/merge.py`\n- `src/troupe/loader.py`\n- `src/troupe/tree.py`\n- `src/troupe/reconcile.py`\n- `src/troupe/paths.py`\n- `tests/unit/test_merge.py`\n- `tests/integration/test_loading_ref.py`\n- `tests/integration/test_reads_overrides.py`

A scalar becomes ``ReplaceScalar``, a\n  mapping or list ``ReplaceCollection`` and a text include ``ReplaceText`` (the target file's\n  content is rewritten; see ``troupe.writeback.apply``).\n- A value whose whole value comes from a YAML include (either form, with or without overrides)\n  is never replaced (FR-012, exit 3); a path *inside* the included file is an ordinary path.\n- *Overrides* (replaces step-1 rule 2): a new key whose deepest existing ancestor comes from an\n  included file is written as an override in the **including file**, the file holding the\n  include reference nearest that ancestor. A scalar-form include reference is first converted\n  to mapping form (``ConvertInclude``); a mapping-form one gets the key in its holder mapping\n  (``AddKeys``), inside an existing override mapping where one exists (FR-005, FR-007).\n- *In-include*: with ``in_include`` a new key goes into the file holding its deepest existing\n  ancestor (the step-1 rule, FR-010), i.e. the innermost included file that defines it.\n- Lists: appending an item (a final index equal to the list's length; larger exits 5) or adding\n  a key inside a list item is written in the file that holds the list (FR-009).\n\nAlgorithm for a new key (research R-3). Let D be the deepest existing ancestor mapping:\n\n1. D is a merged view (both an included file and an override define it): the key goes to D's\n   override side, or with ``in_include`` to its base side (``_on_side`` / ``_on_base``).\n2. D is concrete and ``in_include`` is set, or D is in the root file: ``AddKeys(D, ...)``.\n3. assert the placement holds concrete nodes only.\"\"\"\n    root = _load(make_theatre, files)\n    placement = place(root, parse_path(path), path, in_include=in_include)\n    _assert_concrete(placement)\n    return ...[truncated 14027 chars]

All four gates pass, and the only other test changes are the two expected integration tests, now marked strict xfail.\n\n**Files changed** (all in `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-c`):\n- `src/troupe/writeback/placement.py`:\n  - `place(root, segments, dotted, *, in_include=False)` and the new `ConvertInclude(include, parent, slot, keys)` placement.\n  - `concrete_parent`, which finds an existing node's parent by identity among the concrete sides, never through a merged view.\n  - `_include_ref`, which finds the nearest include reference and applies the list rule.\n  - The new-key paths (`_on_side`, `_on_base`, `_convert`) and the refusals for a `$ref` segment (D-6), a whole include (either form) and a mapping both sides define (D-10).\n  - A rewritten module docstring; the "expected to change at step 5" note is gone.\n- `tests/unit/test_placement_overrides.py` (new): 34 tests that load real theatres and cover every case the prompt lists, plus extra cases for a scalar-form include with a nested route, flow vs block context, replacing a one-sided mapping and appending to an override list. A helper asserts that no placement in the module carries a `MergedMapping`.\n- `tests/integration/test_set_new_entries.py`: this file belongs to WP04, so this is an edit outside my owned files, noted in the commit message. I added `@pytest.mark.xfail(reason="WP04: Overrides replaces rule 2 under set", strict=True)` to `test_new_key_goes_into_included_files_mapping` and `test_new_key_into_empty_included_file`. The file did not import pytest, so it also needed an `import pytest` line, which makes the edit 4 lines rather than one per test. No other existing test failed.\n\n**Gates** (full run from the lane worktree):\n- `uv run pytest -q`: `1718 passed, 2 xfailed in 340.08s`\n- `uv run mypy --strict`: `Success: no issues found in 72 source files`\n- `uv run ruff check`: `All checks passed!`\n- `uv run ruff format --check`: `76 files already formatted`\n\n**Commits and status:**\n- Following the coordinator's request, I first committed a WIP. Nothing changed after it, so instead of adding an empty commit I reworded that unpushed commit into the final one: `e63f759 feat(WP03): place new keys as overrides; add --in-include placement and refusals`.\n- `align-lane.sh` then added `5e2fcf9 chore: align lane spec with planning branch`.\n- `mark-status`: "Marked 5 subtasks as done: T013, T014, T015, T016, T017".\n- `move-task WP03 --to for_review` succeeded with no lane-guard refusal. It printed a dependency alert that WP04 depends on WP03, and the status board shows WP03 in for_review.\n\n**Where I differed from the prompt or the code:**\n1. **T015 steps 1 and 2 share one code path.** The prompt checks "R is the `ref` of a MergedMapping" before "R has a holder". The loader always makes that MergedMapping's `override` the same object as `R.holder`, and a holder with only `$ref` has no route keys to descend into. So both cases go through `_on_side(root, R.holder, route, keys)` and give the same result; a comment explains this. The tests cover the ref-only holder, the merged holder and existing override mappings at two depths.\n2. **`ConvertInclude` fields differ between documents.** I followed the WP prompt (`include, parent, slot, keys`); `data-model.md` lists only `ConvertInclude(include, keys)`. `parent=None` means the include is the whole document of its file.\n3. **`_on_side` also descends through include-valued overrides.** The prompt only says "or `_on_side` applies". When an existing override value on the route is a scalar-form include, the result is a `ConvertInclude` of that include with the rest of the route; when it is a mapping-form include, the descent continues in its holder.\n4. **The D-10 message names files via `unwrap(side).origin.file`.** For a nested merged side, that is the merged view's origin, which is the file holding the override.

I found nothing that blocks it.\n\n**Commands run, with result lines**\n- Review prompt read. `git log` / `git diff method/mission-troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4..HEAD`: the WP03 commit `e63f759` touches only `src/troupe/writeback/placement.py`, `tests/unit/test_placement_overrides.py` (new) and `tests/integration/test_set_new_entries.py`. That last file gets only the declared edit: two `xfail(strict=True)` markers plus `import pytest`. The other files in the range diff come from the WP01 lane-a merge, not from WP03.\n- `uv run pytest -q tests/unit/test_placement_overrides.py tests/unit/test_paths.py tests/integration/test_set_new_entries.py tests/integration/test_set_existing.py` gave `130 passed, 2 xfailed in 42.13s`.\n- `uv run mypy --strict` gave `Success: no issues found in 72 source files`.\n- `uv run ruff check` gave `All checks passed!`. `uv run ruff format --check` gave `76 files already formatted`. I did not run the full pytest suite.\n- `align-lane.sh` gave `aligned: bfa134a`. `method agent tasks move-task WP03 --to approved ...` gave `Moved WP03 from in_review to approved`.\n\n**Hand traces (all line references are to placement.py)**\n- **US2-1** (scalar-form `coder`, new key): `_include_ref` finds the include at depth 1, which has no holder, so `_convert` runs. `concrete_parent` returns the root mapping and slot `coder`, giving `ConvertInclude` in troupe.yml. Correct.\n- **US2-4:**\n  - Override `limits` absent: R is the ref, and the loader sets `deep_merge(base=include, ...)` with `ref=include`, so `_peel` yields it. Then `_on_side(holder, ["limits"], ["min"])` gives `AddKeys(holder, ["limits","min"])`.\n  - Override `limits` present: D is a `MergedMapping`, so `_on_side(D.override)` gives `AddKeys(override limits, ["min"])`.\n  - Override present, deeper key: the walk descends through the existing override mapping. Correct in all three.\n- **US2-5:** D is base.yaml's root. The nearest reference targeting base.yaml is the `model` include at depth 2, which lives in agents/coder.yaml. Result: `ConvertInclude`, parent in coder.yaml, slot `model`. This matches the PO decision (DM 01M46FYYFFXXZE893KWDP2WJCW).\n- **US3-4:** a `SequenceNode` among `found.nodes[depth:k]` makes `_include_ref` return None, so the result is `AddKeys(D)` in coder.yaml.\n- **US4-2:** with `in_include` and a concrete D, the result is `AddKeys(D)` in base.yaml, the innermost file. `_on_base` unwraps include chains and recurses into `.base` for merged roots, as D-8 requires.\n- **D-10 (lines 258–263):** the message names PATH plus the base and override files. **D-6 (line 164):** checked before the walk, for any segment.\n- **`in_include` scope:** it is read only after the existing-value branch (line 169), the list-append branch (lines 174–184) and the digit-segment check. It cannot change existing values, appends or refusals, and the tests run both flag values for replace and append.\n- **Concrete parents and placements:** every parent comes from `concrete_parent`, which searches by identity over the base and override sides and plain mappings. It never reads `MergedMapping.entries`, and nothing in `src/` uses `unwrap(nodes[-2])`. The test helper `_assert_concrete` checks every placement field in the suite.\n- **Refusals:** each one raises `WriteRefused`, and `_refusal` asserts `exit_code == 3` and that the message contains PATH.\n\n**Implementer notes**\n1. **Unified `_on_side` path:** acceptable. Because base is the ref, `holder` is the merged top's `override`, so behaviour matches R-3 for a ref-only holder, a merged holder, and existing override mappings along the route.\n2. **`ConvertInclude` signature:** fine.\n3. **Descending through include-valued overrides:** consistent with the nearest-reference rule. The reference that targets D's file is still the one chosen, and the descent only decides where in that file's holder the keys go.\n4. **D-10 file names:** correct.\n5. **Out-of-map xfail edit:** as declared.\n\n**Anti-pattern checklist:** all eight pass, except item 7 (shared-file ownership), which is noted below.\n- Dead code: none. `concrete_parent` and `ConvertInclude` are used by `place`.\n- No synthetic fixtures: tests load real theatres through the loader.\n- No silent empty returns: the `None` return from `_include_ref` is documented.\n- FR-005, FR-007..FR-010 and FR-012 are all covered by tests.\n- No frozen files touched, and no MUST NOT clause contradicted.\n- The two `assert`s are invariants that hold under `deep_merge`.\n- Item 7: `test_set_new_entries.py` is WP04-owned. The coordination note is in the commit message.\n\n**Non-blocking findings**\n- `placement.py:328` and `placement.py:340` use `assert` for the rule that override and base sides are mappings. Given how `deep_merge` works this can't be reached, but if a future merge rule broke it, the user would see an `AssertionError` instead of exit 3 or 5.\n- Consequence of the nearest-reference rule: suppose an outer override (say in troupe.yml) already defines `coder.model`, and the inner base of `model` is a scalar include written in coder.yaml. A new key under a one-sided sub-mapping of the include's content is then converted in coder.yaml, not added to troupe.yml's override. This follows the DM and reads back correctly; I mention it only so the WP04 and WP05 integration tests are aware of it.\n- WP04 must remove the two strict xfail markers in `tests/integration/test_set_new_entries.py`.\n- The lane worktree has an untracked `.method/` directory, which this WP did not create. I left it alone.\n\n**Relevant files**\n- /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-c/src/troupe/writeback/placement.py\n- /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-c/tests/unit/test_placement_overrides.py\n- /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-c/tests/integration/test_set_new_entries.py

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"``set --in-include`` (US4), through the real CLI and real files.\n\nContract *In-include* (spec FR-010, FR-011, D-8, D-9): with ``--in-include`` a new key goes into\nthe file holding its deepest existing ancestor, the innermost included file that defines it (the\nstep-1 rule); existing values change where they live, exactly as without the flag; the flag is\naccepted only by ``set``, anywhere among its arguments, and combines with ``--reason``. Every\nsuccessful test compares the bytes of every theatre file.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom conftest import CliResult, MakeTheatre, history_events, run_cli, snapshot\n\ntype Files = dict[str, str | bytes]\n\nCODER = \"agents/coder.yaml\"\nCONTRACT_EXAMPLE: Files = {\n    \"troupe.yml\": \"coder: file:agents/coder.yaml  # c\\n\",\n    CODER: \"model: gpt\\n\",\n}\n\n\ndef _set(theatre: Path, *args: str, env: dict[str, str] | None = None) -> CliResult:\n    return run_cli(\"config\", \"set\", *args, \"--theatre\", str(theatre), env=env)\n\n\ndef _get(theatre: Path, path: str) -> str:\n    result = run_cli(\"config\", \"get\", path, \"--theatre\", str(theatre))\n    assert (result.code, result.err) == (0, \"\"), result.err\n    return result.out\n\n\ndef _bytes(files: Files) -> dict[str, bytes]:\n    return {\n        f\".troupe/{rel}\": content.encode(\"utf-8\") if isinstance(content, str) else content\n        for rel, content in files.items()\n    }\n\n\ndef _check_in_include(\n    make_theatre: MakeTheatre,\n    files: Files,\n    path: str,\n    value: str,\n    expected: Files,\n    shown: dict[str, str],\n) -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Build ``files``, run ``set --in-include``, assert every file's bytes and ``get``.\"\"\"\n    theatre = make_theatre(files)\n    result = _set(theatre, \"--in-include\", path, value)\n    assert (result.code, result.out, result.err) == (0, \"\", \"\")\n    assert snapshot(theatre) == _bytes({**files, **expected})\n    for leaf, printed in shown.item...[truncated 4763 chars]

assistant: WP04 is finished and in the for_review column. All four gates pass. One of your instructions can't be met as written: the CLI-level anchor/tag test can't get exit 3, because the loader already rejects anchors and tags with exit 4 before `set` reaches the converter (see deviation 1).\n\n**Files changed** (lane-d worktree `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-d`):\n- `src/troupe/writeback/apply.py` (T018/T019):\n  - `apply_set(..., *, in_include=False)` passes the flag to `place`.\n  - A `ConvertInclude` placement is now handed to `convert_include_edits`.\n  - New `_concrete` asserts stop any `MergedMapping` from reaching a writer (`ConvertInclude.parent`, `AddKeys`, `AppendItem`, `ReplaceCollection`, `ReplaceScalar`).\n  - The module docstring is updated.\n  - `_same_outside` now has a narrow allowance, applied only on the route to PATH (a strict prefix). An old include, scalar or `$ref`-only form, may become a merged view whose `ref.target.path` is the same; its content must be a plain mapping. A plain included mapping may become a nested merged view (`ref=None`) whose base is that same mapping (same file and start offset).\n  - Everywhere else the check got stricter: a merged view must keep the same ref target (or both none), a plain mapping must not become merged, and a YAML or text include must keep its form (scalar vs mapping).\n- `src/troupe/cli.py` and `src/troupe/commands/set_value.py` (T020): `set --in-include` is added and passed through; docstrings are updated. History recording is unchanged.\n- `src/troupe/writeback/collections.py` (out-of-map, both extra items):\n  - **A:** `REF_KEY` is now imported from `troupe.merge`. There is no import cycle: `merge.py` imports only `paths` and `tree`.\n  - **B:** new `_refuse_properties`. Before converting, it raises `WriteRefused` (naming PATH and the file) if anything other than blanks or comments sits between the include's owner and the include string. The owner is the key's `:`, the item's `-`, the previous flow item, or the start of the document, where directives and `---` are allowed.\n- `tests/integration/test_set_new_entries.py` (T021): both xfails and the now-unused `import pytest` are removed. The two tests are renamed with the contract expectations, two `--in-include` twins are added, and the comment you specified is above them. `_check`/`_set` gained an optional flag. No `xfail` remains (grep count 0).\n\n**Tests added:**\n- `tests/integration/test_set_overrides.py`:\n  - **US2:** cases 1, 3, 4 and 8, plus an extra test for a nested key under a mapping form that already has overrides.\n  - **US3:** cases 1 to 5.\n  - **US3-6:** 6 parametrized cases (3 include forms × whole value or `$ref`). Each checks exit 3, no file changed and no events of its own.\n  - **No-op test:** a new override is written and recorded once; setting the same value again writes and records nothing.\n  - **Anchor/tag:** the CLI test `test_include_with_anchor_or_tag_is_not_converted`, plus direct converter tests over 6 layouts × anchor/tag (refusals) and the same 6 layouts without them (conversions accepted).\n  - **Verifier:** checks call `apply_set` with a monkeypatched `_edits` that returns crafted text. The legitimate conversion passes. These are refused: a different target on the route, an off-route form change, an off-route override on the same target, an off-route merged view with a different target, an included mapping turned into a merged view off the route, and a plain mapping turned into a merged view.\n  - I checked the verifier and anchor tests by mutation: removing the route condition, the include-form check, the same-target check, or the anchor/tag refusal each makes at least one test fail.\n- `tests/integration/test_set_in_include.py`: US4 cases 1 to 6, including get/show/history/watch each exiting 2, and the flag accepted before, between or after the arguments.\n\n**Gates** (last runs):\n- `uv run pytest -q`: `1806 passed in 359.21s (0:05:59)`\n- `uv run mypy --strict`: `Success: no issues found in 74 source files`\n- `uv run ruff check`: `All checks passed!`\n- `uv run ruff format --check`: `78 files already formatted`\n\n**Commits:** I squashed the WIP commits (6ed3dc7, d9722de, 6533893) into `6f2a282 feat(WP04): set --in-include, include conversion wiring and narrow verifier allowance`. Its message gives the reasons and mentions TROUPE-007 and tracker crs. On top is `7d7f396`, the commit `align-lane.sh` made. Nothing is untracked and no `__pycache__` is tracked.\n\n**Status:** `mark-status` marked all 6 subtasks (T018 to T023) done. `move-task WP04 --to for_review` was accepted on the first try, without `--force` or a lane-guard refusal, and the status board shows WP04 in for_review.\n\n**Deviations and contradictions:**\n1. **Item B can't produce exit 3 through the CLI.** `yamlcore._convert` rejects any node whose span starts with `&` or `!`, so `coder: &a file:x.yaml` or `coder: !t file:x.yaml` exits 4 ("anchors and tags are not supported") before `set` gets to conversion. I checked this by hand. So `test_include_with_anchor_or_tag_is_not_converted` asserts exit 4, empty stdout and no file changed. The exit-3 refusal in `convert_include_edits` is a backstop; its tests call the converter directly with a crafted text. The orchestrator needs to decide whether D-12 should describe it that way. Choice recorded for the handoff: refuse any non-blank, non-comment text before the include string, which is broader than only `&` or `!` tokens; I didn't close tracker crs.\n2. **I widened the T019 allowance for US2-4.** The prompt only allowed include → merged view. US2-4 (`set coder.limits.min 1` with `limits` in the included file) also turns a plain included mapping into a nested merged view with no ref. I allowed that only on the route and only when the base is the same mapping (same file and offset). Without it, US2-4 would be refused.\n3. **The `--in-include`-equals-plain-set test reuses one directory.** `make_theatre` always builds the same theatre directory, so `test_in_include_without_include_equals_plain_set` snapshots the flagged run, then rebuilds the directory from the same files for the plain run. The history store carries over, but it doesn't affect the byte comparison.
assistant: WP04 is done with all four gates passing (1806 tests). The implementer found that the anchor/tag risk can't actually be reached from the CLI: since step 1, the loader has rejected anchors and tags with exit 4 before `set` ever gets to conversion. If `--in-include` adds a key to the *included* file while the view already has override-only keys, the new key appears in the middle of the merged key list, not at the end. The verifier only accepts `[*old_keys, added]`, so it refuses a correct rendering with exit 3. FR-010 is broken in this case, and so is the mission's own quickstart.\n\n**Reproduction (quickstart.md run verbatim):**\n\n```\nprintf 'coder: file:agents/coder.yaml  # c\\n' > $T/.troupe/troupe.yml\nprintf 'model: gpt\\ntemperature: 0.7\\n' > $T/.troupe/agents/coder.yaml\ntroupe config set coder.top_p 0.9 --theatre $T                  # exit 0\ntroupe config set coder.temperature 0.5 --theatre $T            # exit 0\ntroupe config set coder.top_p 0.8 --theatre $T                  # exit 0\ntroupe config set --in-include coder.max_tokens 100 --theatre $T\n# -> troupe: coder.max_tokens: cannot write this change byte-surgically; no file changed  (exit 3)\n```\n\nExpected (quickstart): `max_tokens: 100` is appended to `coder.yaml`, and `show coder --sources` lists `model`, `temperature`, `max_tokens` from coder.yaml and `top_p` from troupe.yml.\n\nWith `_same_outside` bypassed, the rendering is correct: `coder.yaml` becomes `model: gpt\\nmax_tokens: 100\\n`, and the merged key order is `['model', 'max_tokens', 'top_p']` against the old `['model', 'top_p']`. assistant: **Verdict: rejected.** WP04 is moved back to planned with feedback in `/tmp/feedback-include-overrides-WP04.md`. The mission's own quickstart fails at its `--in-include` step: a correct write is refused with exit 3.\n\n**Blocking issue**\n\nThe problem is in the key-order check in `_same_outside` in `src/troupe/writeback/apply.py`:\n```python\nif added is None or added in old_keys or new_keys != [*old_keys, added]:\n    return False\n```\n- **Cause:** a merged view lists the included file's keys first, then keys that exist only as overrides (FR-002). When `--in-include` adds a key to the included file while such override-only keys exist, the new key lands in the middle of the list. The check only accepts it at the end, so it refuses a correct write. FR-010 is broken in this case.\n- **Repro (quickstart.md run as written):** `set coder.top_p 0.9`, `set coder.temperature 0.5` and `set coder.top_p 0.8` all exit 0. Then `set --in-include coder.max_tokens 100` gives `troupe: coder.max_tokens: cannot write this change byte-surgically; no file changed`, exit 3.\n- **Root cause confirmed:** with the check patched out, the edit is correct (`coder.yaml` becomes `model: gpt\nmax_tokens: 100\n`). The new key order is `['model', 'max_tokens', 'top_p']` against the old `['model', 'top_p']`.\n- **Nested case fails the same way:** `coder: {$ref: file:c.yaml, limits: {x: 1}}` with `c.yaml` containing `limits: {a: 1}`, then `set --in-include coder.limits.b 2`, exits 3.\n- **Control passes:** when every override key also exists in the included file, `set --in-include coder.b 2` exits 0.\n- **No test covers it:** none of the US4 tests uses `--in-include` on an include that already has override-only keys.\n- **Fix requested:**\n  - When both old and new are merged views on the route, also accept the new key at the end of the included-key block; keep everything else strict.\n  - Add CLI story tests for the flat and nested cases, plus a crafted-output test showing any other insert position is still refused.\n\n**Manual CLI checks** (`uv run python -m troupe config ... --theatre <tmp>`)\n- **Contract example:** `set coder.temperature 0.3` exits 0. `troupe.yml` is exactly `'coder:  # c\n  $ref: file:agents/coder.yaml\n  temperature: 0.3\n'` and `cmp` shows `coder.yaml` unchanged.\n- **`--in-include` on a scalar-form include:** appends to `coder.yaml` only (`'model: m\ntemperature: 0.3\ntop_p: 0.9\n'`) and `troupe.yml` is unchanged.\n- **Overridden value:** `set coder.temperature 0.4` changes only the override and `coder.yaml` is unchanged. `--in-include coder.temperature 0.6` also changes only the override.\n- **Value not overridden:** it is written into `coder.yaml`.\n- **No-op:** setting a value to its current value changes no file and records no event.\n- **Replacing the whole include:** `set coder '{a: 1}'` exits 3 and no file changes.\n- **History:** one `set` event per change, and the first override shows `"old": null`.\n- **`--in-include` on `get`, `show`, `history` and `watch`:** all exit 2 with `troupe: unrecognized arguments: --in-include ...`.\n- **Anchor/tag claim (deviation 1):** `coder: &a file:x.yaml` and `coder: !t file:x.yaml` both exit 4 ("anchors and tags are not supported") and the file is unchanged. The claim holds.\n\n**Gates**\n- Full `uv run pytest -q`: `1806 passed in 366.51s`. This covers the four targeted test files, so I didn't run them separately.\n- `uv run mypy --strict`: `Success: no issues found in 74 source files`.\n- `uv run ruff check`: `All checks passed!`\n- `uv run ruff format --check`: `78 files already formatted`.\n\n**Checklist results**\n- **T018–T023:** all delivered.\n- **Wiring:** `ConvertInclude` is reached from the real `set` path, which my contract-example run went through, so there is no dead code.\n- **Writer guards:** writes through a merged view are asserted against, except for `ConvertInclude.parent` when it is `None`, which is fine.\n- **Step-1 tests:** no `xfail` is left in `test_set_new_entries.py`. Only the two step-1 tests changed expectations, and their `--in-include` twins keep the step-1 output.\n- **Story tests and refusals:** story tests compare every file's bytes. Refusal tests assert that no file changed and that no events were recorded.\n- **Deviation 2 (verifier allowance):** acceptable. It applies only on the route and only for the same target, or for the same base mapping (same file and start offset). The crafted-output tests show other targets and off-route changes are refused.\n- **Deviation 3 (rebuilt theatre dir):** sound, because the first run's snapshot is taken before the rebuild.\n- **Out-of-map edits:** the `REF_KEY` import and `_refuse_properties` in `collections.py` are fine.\n- **Anti-pattern list:** dead code, synthetic fixtures, frozen surface and fragility all PASS. FR coverage FAILs for FR-010 because of the defect above.\n\n**Non-blocking**\n- **Possible verifier blind spot:** when a merged view is compared with a merged view, only the `$ref` target and the merged entries are checked. A value moving between the included file and the override, with the same merged result, would not be caught. Placement prevents this today and it is not a regression in this WP. A follow-up tracker is suggested.\n- **Docstring:** the `_gains_override` docstring says "that file is not the one being written", but the code doesn't check it. Either remove the sentence or add the check.\n\n**Other notes**\n- WP05 depends on WP04 and will need to rebase after the fix.\n- The move reset subtasks T018–T023 to planned, so they need to be re-marked done before the WP goes back to review.\n\nRelevant files:\n- `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-d/src/troupe/writeback/apply.py`\n- `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-d/tests/integration/test_set_in_include.py`\n- `/work/repo/spec/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4/quickstart.md`\n- `/tmp/feedback-include-overrides-WP04.md`

The quickstart's own `--in-include` step fails with exit 3:\n\n1. **The trigger:** the include already has override keys that don't exist in the included file, and you add a new key to the included file with `--in-include`.\n2. **Why it's refused:** the merged view lists the included file's keys first, so the new key lands in the middle of the key list. The pre-write check only accepts a new key at the end, so it refuses a correct write.\n3. **Impact:** it's safe (nothing is written) but wrong, and it breaks FR-010.\n\nI'm re-claiming WP04 and sending the fix back to the same implementer, who has the context. I'm also filing the reviewer's non-blocking verifier blind spot as a follow-up tracker.

WP04 is re-claimed in lane-d; the regenerated prompt (with feedback) is at /tmp/method-prompts/07e9f96d21056a26/method-implement-troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-WP04.md and the full feedback is /tmp/feedback-include-overrides-WP04.md — read both.\n\nBlocking issue: `apply._same_outside` (MappingNode branch) only accepts the added key at the END of the key list (`new_keys == [*old_keys, added]`). A merged view lists base keys first, then override-only keys, so `set --in-include coder.max_tokens 100` on `coder: {$ref: file:agents/coder.yaml, top_p: 0.8}` (coder.yaml without top_p) is falsely refused with exit 3 — the quickstart fails. Nested form fails too (`coder: {$ref: file:c.yaml, limits: {x: 1}}`, c.yaml `limits: {a: 1}`, `set --in-include coder.limits.b 2`).\n\nRequired fix: when old and new are both MergedMapping on the route, accept `added` at the end of the base-key block or at the very end (derive the expected order from the merge rule / sides); keep plain mappings and everything off the route strict. Add CLI story tests in tests/integration/test_set_in_include.py for the flat and nested cases (snapshot every file; coder.yaml/c.yaml gets the key, troupe.yml byte-identical), plus a crafted-output test that any other insertion position is still refused. assistant: The blocking review issue is fixed: `set --in-include` now works for a new key under an include that already has override-only keys. WP04 is back in for_review, the quickstart runs end to end, and all four gates pass.\n\n**Cause (two problems in `_same_outside` in `src/troupe/writeback/apply.py`):**\n- It only accepted the added key at the very end of the key list. A merged view lists the included file's keys first and then the override-only keys, so a key added to the included file lands at the end of the included block, not at the end.\n- It paired old and new entries by position. Once the key landed in the middle, every later entry was compared with the wrong one. Fixing the key order alone wasn't enough; I found this second problem by tracing the refusal.\n\n**Fix:**\n- A new helper `_orders` lists the allowed key orders. A plain mapping can only gain the key at the end. A merged view can also gain it at the end of its included block, but only when the included file's last key is the added key, so the position follows the merge rule rather than being loosened.\n- Old and new entries are now paired by key. Plain mappings and everything off the route are checked exactly as before.\n- I removed the unchecked claim from the `_gains_override` docstring. It now says only what the code checks (same file, same position).\n\n**Tests added:**\n- In `tests/integration/test_set_in_include.py` (each snapshots every file):\n  - Flat case: `coder: {$ref: file:agents/coder.yaml, top_p: 0.8}` plus `set --in-include coder.max_tokens 100`. Only `coder.yaml` changes.\n  - Nested case: `coder: {$ref: file:c.yaml, limits: {x: 1}}` plus `set --in-include coder.limits.b 2`. Only `c.yaml` changes.\n  - `test_quickstart_runs_end_to_end`: the full quickstart sequence, including the exact `show --sources` output.\n- In `tests/integration/test_set_overrides.py`, crafted-output tests. The correct position passes. These are still refused: the new key in the middle of the included keys, at the start of them, or placed in the override before `top_p`.\n\n**Quickstart (run verbatim on a temp theatre; every command exited 0):**\n- After `set coder.top_p 0.9`: `troupe.yml` is `coder:  # c` / `  $ref: file:agents/coder.yaml` / `  top_p: 0.9`, and `coder.yaml` is unchanged.\n- `show coder --sources` printed:\n  ```\n  model: gpt # from .troupe/agents/coder.yaml\n  temperature: 0.5 # from .troupe/agents/coder.yaml\n  max_tokens: 100 # from .troupe/agents/coder.yaml\n  top_p: 0.8 # from .troupe/troupe.yml\n  ```\n- `history` shows 2 imports, then 4 `set` events: `coder.top_p` (null→0.9), `coder.temperature` (0.7→0.5), `coder.top_p` (0.9→0.8), `coder.max_tokens` (null→100).\n- Final files: `troupe.yml` is `coder:  # c` / `  $ref: file:agents/coder.yaml` / `  top_p: 0.8`. `coder.yaml` is `model: gpt` / `temperature: 0.5` / `max_tokens: 100`.\n\n**Gates:**\n- `uv run pytest -q`: `1813 passed in 362.79s (0:06:02)`\n- `uv run mypy --strict`: `Success: no issues found in 74 source files`\n- `uv run ruff check`: `All checks passed!`\n- `uv run ruff format --check`: `78 files already formatted`\n- No `xfail` remains in `test_set_new_entries.py`.\n\n**Commits:** I squashed the two WIP commits (a3d14ea, 66115ee) into `0c852f0 fix(WP04): verifier accepts a new included key before override-only keys`. Its message explains why and ends with the Co-Authored-By line. `align-lane.sh` then added `1e09a36`. The working tree is clean.\n\n**Status:**\n- T018–T023 are marked done again.\n- The prompt has no separate feedback-acknowledgement command; the move-task note records the fix.\n- `move-task WP04 --to for_review` was accepted without `--force`, and WP04 is in For Review. It also printed "Pre-review regression gate: no_coverage — no gate contract for … in_progress->for_review"; this is informational and didn't block the move.\n\n**Follow-up:** I filed tracker `repo` (P3) for the reviewer's non-blocking note. When both sides are merged views, the verifier doesn't check which side each key comes from, so a value moving between the override and the included file with an identical merged result wouldn't be caught. Placement prevents this today.\n\n**Deviations:**\n- The new-key position allowance is applied to every new merged view on the route, including one that is just gaining its first override. The review only asked for the case where both old and new are merged views. It's the same position rule, so it adds nothing beyond what the merge rule allows.\n- The three points from cycle 1 still apply. The anchor/tag case exits 4 at the CLI because the loader rejects it before the converter's exit-3 check; D-12 still needs wording. The plain-mapping-to-nested-merged-view allowance needed for US2-4 is unchanged. The `--in-include`-equals-plain-set test still reuses one theatre directory.
