assistant: **Verdict: WP04 approved (cycle 2).** The lane was aligned first, at commit c58becc, and WP04 is now `approved`. Both cycle-1 failures now work through the real CLI.\n\n**Code review (`git diff 6f2a282..HEAD -- src tests`)**\n- **Key order:** the new `_orders` helper allows two orders when a mapping gains a key: `[*old_keys, added]`, or, only for a merged view whose included side's last key is the added key, the new key at the end of the included-key block. This matches the merge rule (included keys first, then override-only keys) and is no broader than that.\n- **Pairing by key:** this can't hide a dropped or duplicated key. When the key lists differ, `new_keys` must exactly equal one of the `_orders` lists, and each of those is all of `old_keys` plus exactly one `added` key that isn't already in `old_keys`. When the lists are equal, pairing by key is the same as pairing by position.\n- **First-override case:** when a node gets its first override, the included side doesn't change, so its last key can't be the added key. Only the "at the end" order applies there.\n- **Unchanged parts:** plain mappings and everything off the route behave as before. The `_gains_override` docstring is fixed.\n- **New tests:** flat and nested `--in-include` story tests that check every file's bytes, `test_quickstart_runs_end_to_end`, and crafted-output tests showing a new key at any other position in a merged view is refused. No `xfail` remains in the three test files.\n\n**Manual CLI checks**\n- **Quickstart, run as written:** all four `set` commands exit 0.\n  - `set --in-include coder.max_tokens 100` now exits 0.\n  - `troupe.yml` is `'coder:  # c\n  $ref: file:agents/coder.yaml\n  top_p: 0.8\n'` and `coder.yaml` is `'model: gpt\ntemperature: 0.5\nmax_tokens: 100\n'`.\n  - `show coder --sources` lists `model`, `temperature` and `max_tokens` from coder.yaml and `top_p` from troupe.yml, as the quickstart expects.\n- **Override after the in-include insert:** `set coder.extra 1` exits 0, appends `extra: 1` to troupe.yml, and `cmp` shows `coder.yaml` unchanged.\n- **Nested repro:** `set --in-include coder.limits.b 2` exits 0. `c.yaml` becomes `'limits:\n  a: 1\n  b: 2\n'` and `troupe.yml` is unchanged. Sources show `a` and `b` from c.yaml and `x` from troupe.yml.\n- **Other subcommands:** `get --in-include ...` exits 2 with "unrecognized arguments: --in-include".\n\n**Gates**\n- `uv run pytest -q`: `1813 passed in 364.38s`.\n- `uv run mypy --strict`: `Success: no issues found in 74 source files`.\n- `uv run ruff check`: `All checks passed!`\n- `uv run ruff format --check`: `78 files already formatted`.\n\n**Non-blocking findings**\n1. **Chained mapping-form includes:** `--in-include` still refuses safely here. Repro:\n   - `troupe.yml` = `coder: {$ref: file:c.yaml, top_p: 1}`\n   - `c.yaml` = `$ref: file:b.yaml` plus `mid: 1`\n   - `b.yaml` = `model: gpt`\n   - `set --in-include coder.newk 2` exits 3 with "cannot write this change byte-surgically", and no file changes.\n\n   `_orders` only looks one level into the included side, so a key added deeper lands mid-list and is refused. The no-flag path on the same fixture works (exit 0, override appended). It fails safe and no spec scenario covers it, so I'm suggesting a follow-up tracker rather than blocking; I didn't create one because I stayed read-only.\n2. **Verifier blind spot:** the gap from cycle 1 (merged views compared without checking which side each key comes from) is tracked by tracker c22.\n\nWP05 depends on WP04 and can proceed.\n\nRelevant files:\n- `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-d/src/troupe/writeback/apply.py`\n- `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-d/tests/integration/test_set_in_include.py`\n- `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-d/tests/integration/test_set_overrides.py`

tool: Write {"content": "\"\"\"Acceptance for include overrides: edges, refusals, history and watch (WP05).\n\nEverything runs through the real CLI against real files and the real event store. Successful\nwrites compare the bytes of **every** theatre file before and after; refusals check the exit\ncode, an empty stdout, a one-line stderr naming PATH or the file, that no file changed and that\nno event was recorded (NFR-003).\n\nCovered here (spec references in each section): conversion edges (US2-2, US2-5 to US2-7, D-3 to\nD-5, CRLF), refusals (D-1, D-6, D-10, D-11), history and reconcile (FR-013, step-2 rules),\n``watch``, and chained mapping-form includes (``--in-include`` into the innermost file, D-8).\nAlready covered elsewhere and not repeated: the contract example and quickstart\n(``test_set_overrides.py``, ``test_set_in_include.py``), and includes written with an anchor or\ntag (``test_set_overrides.py``: exit 4 at load, converter backstop exit 3).\n\"\"\"\n\nimport time\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\nfrom conftest import (\n    CliResult,\n    MakeTheatre,\n    WatchProcess,\n    core,\n    history_events,\n    recorded_within,\n    run_cli,\n    snapshot,\n)\nfrom troupe.errors import WriteRefused\nfrom troupe.loader import load\nfrom troupe.paths import parse_path\nfrom troupe.tree import SourceFile\nfrom troupe.values import Value, parse_value\nfrom troupe.writeback import TextEdit, apply\nfrom troupe.writeback.apply import WritePlan, apply_set\nfrom troupe.writeback.placement import AddKeys, Placement, place\n\ntype Files = dict[str, str | bytes]\ntype Event = dict[str, object]\n\nCODER = \"agents/coder.yaml\"\n\n\ndef _set(theatre: Path, *args: str) -> CliResult:\n    return run_cli(\"config\", \"set\", *args, \"--theatre\", str(theatre))\n\n\ndef _get(theatre: Path, path: str) -> str:\n    result = run_cli(\"config\", \"get\", path, \"--theatre\", str(theatre))\n    assert (result.code, result.err) == (0, \"\"), result.err\n    return result.out\n\n...[truncated 19329 chars]

tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-e && python3 - <<'EOF'\np='tests/integration/test_overrides_acceptance.py'\ns=open(p).read()\nstart=s.index('    root = load(config)\\n    target = (config.parent / written).resolve()')\nend=s.index('    def crafted(placement')\nnew='''    root = load(config)\n    path = (config.parent / written).resolve()\n    file = SourceFile(path, path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n\n'''\ns=s[:start]+new+s[end:]\ns=s.replace('\"\"\"Run ``apply_set --in-include coder.newk 2`` with ``written``\\'s new text forced.\"\"\"',\n '\"\"\"Run ``apply_set --in-include coder.newk 2`` with ``written``\\'s new text forced.\\n\\n    Verification reads only ``path`` and the new text of each written file.\\n    \"\"\"')\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nuv run pytest -q tests/integration/test_overrides_acceptance.py 2>&1 | tail -30"}
assistant: Red only on the two c3z tests, as expected. Now the fix in `_orders`.
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-e && python3 - <<'EOF'\np='src/troupe/writeback/apply.py'\ns=open(p).read()\nold=s[s.index('def _orders('):s.index('def _gains_override(')]\nnew='''def _orders(old_keys: list[str], added: str, new: MappingNode) -> list[list[str]]:\n    \"\"\"The key orders ``new`` may have when it gains the key ``added`` (on the route only).\n\n    A plain mapping gains it at the end. A merged view lists its base keys first, then the\n    override-only keys (``troupe.merge``), and so does each merged view nested as a base (chained\n    mapping-form includes, tracker c3z), so the base keys of every level form a prefix of the view's\n    keys. A key added to a base side (``--in-include``, D-8) therefore sits at the end of one of\n    those nested base blocks: allowed only where that level's base side ends with ``added``. A key\n    added as an override is override-only, so it sits at the very end.\n    \"\"\"\n    orders = [[*old_keys, added]]\n    view: MappingNode = new\n    while isinstance(view, MergedMapping):\n        base = unwrap(view.base)\n        if not isinstance(base, MappingNode):  # pragma: no cover - the loader checks (D-7)\n            break\n        if base.entries and base.entries[-1].key == added:\n            at = len(base.entries) - 1\n            orders.append([*old_keys[:at], added, *old_keys[at:]])\n        view = base\n    return orders\n\n\n'''\ns=s.replace(old,new)\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\nuv run pytest -q tests/integration/test_overrides_acceptance.py 2>&1 | tail -3"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-e && git diff --stat; uv run ruff format tests/integration/test_overrides_acceptance.py src/troupe/writeback/apply.py; uv run ruff check; uv run mypy --strict 2>&1 | tail -3"}
tool: Bash {"command": "cd /work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-e && git add tests/integration/test_overrides_acceptance.py src/troupe/writeback/apply.py && git commit -q -F - <<'EOF'\nfix(WP05): accept --in-include keys under chained mapping-form includes; add acceptance tests\n\n`set --in-include coder.newk 2` where troupe.yml's mapping-form include points at a\nfile that is itself a mapping-form include was refused (exit 3): placement correctly\ntargets the innermost included file (D-8, R-3 `_on_base`), but the verifier's\n`_orders` only looked one level into the merged view's base side, so the key at the\nend of the innermost base block looked misplaced. `_orders` now follows the merge\nrule through every nested base (the key may sit only at the end of one of those base\nblocks, or at the very end); everything else stays strict.\n\nOut-of-map edit (tracker repo): src/troupe/writeback/apply.py\n`_orders` only — the fix belongs where the verifier's order rule lives.\n\nAdds tests/integration/test_overrides_acceptance.py (T024-T026): conversion edges,\nrefusals with files and history untouched, history/reconcile/watch, and the chained\ninclude story, no-flag path and crafted wrong-position refusals.\nEOF\ngit log --oneline -1"}

Existing values,\n  list appends and keys inside list items are unaffected by ``in_include``.\"\"\",\n\"\"\"  (``set --in-include``) it goes into the innermost included file that defines its ancestor\n  (the step-1 rule, D-8). In a merged view the new key may sit only at the very end (an override) or\n  at the end of one of its nested base blocks (``--in-include`` into a base side, D-8; chained\n  mapping-form includes nest several, ``_orders``).\"\"\"),\n]\nfor a,b in reps:\n    assert a in s, a\n    s=s.replace(a,b)\nopen(p,'w').write(s)\nEOF\ngit diff --stat"}

Before this it cited no D-n.\n\n## Tests added (35 cases)\n- **T024 (conversion):**\n  - `test_four_space_file_converts_with_four_space_indentation`\n  - `test_nested_include_override_lands_in_nearest_including_file`\n  - `test_flow_parent_include_converts_in_flow_style`\n  - `test_list_item_include_converts_in_place`\n  - `test_quoted_include_string_is_moved_verbatim` (double and single quotes)\n  - `test_override_value_that_is_itself_an_include_is_converted_in_place`\n  - `test_crlf_including_file_keeps_crlf_after_conversion`\n- **T025 (refusals):** every case checks the exit code, empty stdout, a one-line stderr naming the path or file, that no file changed, and that no event was recorded.\n  - `test_ref_segment_in_path_is_refused` (set exits 3 with and without `--in-include`; get and show exit 5)\n  - `test_replacing_partly_overridden_mapping_is_refused`\n  - `test_replacing_one_sided_mapping_under_merge_is_written_in_its_file` (both the included-file side and the override side)\n  - `test_multiline_include_string_conversion_is_refused` (literal block, folded block, wrapped quoted string)\n  - `test_malformed_ref_makes_every_command_exit_4` (3 malformed forms × get/show/set; no history database is created)\n- **T026 (history and watch):**\n  - `test_override_set_records_exactly_one_event_with_cli_lineage`\n  - `test_hand_written_conversion_without_value_change_records_nothing`\n  - `test_hand_added_override_records_set_for_changed_leaf_only`\n  - `test_watch_records_set_written_override_once`\n- **c3z:**\n  - `test_in_include_under_chained_mapping_form_includes_writes_innermost_file` (CLI story; snapshots every file and checks `show --sources`)\n  - `test_new_key_under_chained_mapping_form_includes_becomes_root_override` (the no-flag path appends to `troupe.yml`)\n  - `test_placement_under_chained_includes_targets_innermost_mapping`\n  - `test_verifier_accepts_new_key_at_end_of_innermost_base_block`\n  - `test_verifier_refuses_new_key_elsewhere_under_chained_includes` (crafted output at three wrong positions)\n\n**Skipped as duplicates of WP04:** the quickstart test, the contract-example conversion, and the anchor/tag tests. The prompt's own list did not include these.\n\n## c3z fix\nPlacement was already right: with `--in-include`, `_on_base` goes down to the innermost base, so it returns `AddKeys` on `b.yaml`'s mapping, as D-8 and R-3 require. The refusal came from the check that re-reads the result before writing (`_same_outside`). Its key-order helper, `_orders`, only looked one level into the merged view's base side. One level down is the merged view of `c.yaml`, whose last key is `mid`, so the new key looked misplaced.\n\n`_orders` now loops through every nested base. The added key is accepted only at the very end of the view or at the end of one of the nested base blocks; everything else stays strict. One consequence: a key placed at the end of `c.yaml` would also pass the order check, because that is the end of a nested base block. Choosing which file gets the key is still placement's job.\n\n## Quickstart output (temp theatre)\nEvery command exited 0 and all expectations in `quickstart.md` held.\n```\ntroupe.yml after first set: coder:  # c / $ref: file:agents/coder.yaml / top_p: 0.9 ; 3 set coder.top_p null→0.9; 4 set coder.temperature 0.7→0.5; 5 set coder.top_p 0.9→0.8; 6 set coder.max_tokens null→100 (via cli)\nfinal: troupe.yml "coder:  # c\n  $ref: file:agents/coder.yaml\n  top_p: 0.8\n"; assistant: **Verdict: approved.** WP05 moved from in_review to approved. Nothing blocks it.\n\n## Commands and results\nRun in `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-e`:\n- `uv run pytest -q`: **1848 passed in 407.84s**, exit 0\n- `uv run pytest -q tests/integration/test_overrides_acceptance.py`: 35 passed\n- `uv run mypy --strict`: Success: no issues found in 75 source files\n- `uv run ruff check`: All checks passed!\n- `uv run ruff format --check`: 79 files already formatted\n- `align-lane.sh`: `aligned: a80c8fe chore: align lane spec with planning branch`\n- `move-task WP05 --to approved`: `✓ Moved WP05 from in_review to approved`\n\n**c3z through the real CLI** (troupe.yml `coder: {$ref: file:c.yaml, top_p: 1}`, c.yaml `$ref: file:b.yaml` + `mid: 1`, b.yaml `model: gpt`):\n- `config set --in-include coder.newk 2`: exit 0. Only b.yaml changed (now `model: gpt\nnewk: 2\n`); troupe.yml and c.yaml unchanged. `show --sources` lists newk as from b.yaml. History has one `set coder.newk old=null new=2 via=cli`.\n- `config set coder.newk 2` (no flag): exit 0. Only troupe.yml changed (`newk: 2` added after `top_p: 1`); c.yaml and b.yaml unchanged. One `set` event.\n\n## The `_orders` change (`src/troupe/writeback/apply.py`)\n- **No dropped or duplicated keys can get through.** Every allowed order is the old keys with the one added key inserted, and the comparison is exact equality. A key that already existed is refused before this check.\n- **Position is checked against each file's structure, not just the final key order.** A key is allowed at a given level only if that level's base ends with it. So a key placed before `mid` in c.yaml is refused, even though the merged order looks the same as the correct write into b.yaml.\n- **Crafted wrong-position tests cover all three files under chained includes:** innermost-first, middle-before-mid and root-before-top_p.\n- **My extra probe** forced the new text of each file directly:\n  - Refused, with and without `--in-include`: a dropped `model`, a wrong value (`newk: 3`), a change to `mid`, and two keys added at once.\n\n## Non-blocking findings\n1. **The verifier doesn't check which file the new key is written to. Placement is the only thing that decides it.** The implementer's caveat is correct: crafted writes of the new key at the end of c.yaml or at the end of troupe.yml are both accepted, with and without `--in-include`. I ran the same probe against the pre-WP05 `apply.py` and it accepted those same two writes, so this isn't a WP05 regression. It's the same kind of gap as tracker c22, which covers values moving between files away from the target path; this is the version on the target path. WP05 adds only one newly accepted location, the end of b.yaml, which is the correct one. Side effect: without the flag, a crafted b.yaml write is now accepted where it used to be refused, but the wrong c.yaml write was already accepted before. Suggest extending c22 (or a new tracker) so the verifier checks that the new key's file matches the file placement chose. I didn't change tracker because I'm read-only here.\n2. **The malformed-`$ref` test checks "no event" by asserting the history store was never created.** That's valid because the config never loads, but it's weaker than the before/after event count the other refusal tests use.\n3. **FR-012's "replacing a whole include exits 3" is tested in WP04's files, not in WP05's.** It is covered by `tests/integration/test_set_overrides.py::test_replacing_whole_include_in_either_form_exits_3_and_changes_nothing` and `tests/unit/test_placement_overrides.py:421`.\n4. **The `acceptance-matrix.json` and WP04 `review-cycle-2.md` deletions in the lane diff came from the lane-guard alignment commit (93ca177),** not from WP05's commits (6b3e5cd, 2b60ec3).\n\n## Test checks\n- **Test names match what they assert.**\n- **Every refusal goes through `_assert_refused`,** which checks the exit code, empty stdout, a single stderr line starting `troupe: ` that names the path or file, an unchanged file snapshot and an unchanged event list.\n- **The watch test** waits with `recorded_within(theatre, set_at)`, the standard 2s window, taking the time right after the `set`. There are no bare sleeps used as assertions. It also checks that watch wrote nothing and exited `(0, "", "")`.\n\n## Anti-pattern checklist\nAll eight pass, or don't apply:\n1. Dead code: PASS (no new public API)\n2. Synthetic fixtures: PASS (everything goes through the real CLI or `apply_set`)\n3. Silent empty return: PASS\n4. FR-006/012/013 coverage: PASS\n5. Frozen files: N/A\n6. Locked decisions: PASS\n7. Shared-file ownership: PASS (the out-of-map `apply.py` edit is explained in the commit message, tracker c3z)\n8. Fragility: PASS (no new `raise`; the loop's `break` is a defensive `pragma: no cover` branch)\n\n## Files\n- `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-e/src/troupe/writeback/apply.py`\n- `/work/repo/.worktrees/troupe-config-include-overrides-01M46FY4-lane-e/tests/integration/test_overrides_acceptance.py`\n- The probe script is `/tmp/wp05probe/probe.py`.

assistant: Both change requests are committed on `main` (merge commit `ded0223`). Nothing was pushed and no pull request was opened.\n\n**What changed for users**\n- **Includes can be written as a mapping.** A mapping with a `$ref` key is an include; its other keys are overrides, deep-merged over the included file. `get`, `show`, `show --sources` and history all read the merged values.\n- **CR-A:** a new setting under a shared section becomes an override in the including file, and the shared file is untouched. A plain `file:` include is first converted to mapping form, keeping its end-of-line comment. Existing values still change where they live. List items stay in the file that holds the list.\n- **CR-B:** `set --in-include PATH VALUE` writes a new key into the included file instead (the step-1 rule).\n- **Refusals:**\n  - exit 4: a `$ref` that isn't an include string, and overrides on a non-YAML include;\n  - exit 3: a path containing `$ref`, replacing a whole include, replacing a mapping that is partly overridden, and converting an include whose `file:` string spans several lines.\n\n**Verification**\n- All four gates pass on `main`'s code: 1848 tests passed, mypy reports no issues, and ruff check and ruff format are clean.\n- A CLI run on `main` gave the contract example byte for byte and left the shared file unchanged.\n- The merged code is identical to the code that was reviewed.\n- All 13 functional requirements are recorded as passing in the acceptance matrix, each with the tests that prove it.\n\n**How it went**\n- Five work packages, each reviewed independently.\n- WP04 was rejected once for a real bug: `--in-include` was refused whenever the include already had override-only keys. The fix was approved on the second pass.\n- A further gap with chained includes, found in that re-review, was fixed in WP05.\n\n**Two places where I followed the last mission's precedent**\n- **Acceptance ran with `--lenient`.** The only check it relaxed is the `docs/` folder convention; this repo has never had a `docs/` folder.\n- **I re-recorded the analysis report twice.** The implement and accept gates saw my later spec edits as making it stale.
